| 112 | Correction of row 70 (phase-space law): row 70 said detection of an off-line u at ANY heat time needs degree d ≳ γ_u/(2δ_u), so low-order sections are PD at all a unless such zeros exist | **ROW 70 WRONG at large a (paper)** | At large a the form is dominated by the lowest frequencies. If u is the second-lowest frequency, degree 1 already detects it (Δ_2(a) ≈ e^{−a(λ₁+Re u)}·2Re(e^{−iaIm u}(u−λ₁)²) changes sign for large a). Consequence: all-time positivity of the first d sections ⇔ (roughly) no off-line zero with N(γ_u) ≲ d or γ_u/δ_u ≲ d, i.e. RH below height ≈ N^{−1}(d). The earlier row-70 claim "RH up to height ≈ cd/2" stands only as an order of magnitude, now via the killing regime. |

| 193 | **Consolidation (rows 189–192) and the positivity-duality lesson** | **AUDIT** | ζ^α powers (zeros scaled, not moved) 189; theta-kernel Φ moments → zero moments (logarithmic map, not positivity-preserving) 190; additive (Φ) + multiplicative (Λ) positivity live on Fourier-dual sides 191; Bochner positive-definiteness of the prime side is a convolution property, while zero positivity is a multiplication property 192. Lesson for any future positivity attempt: the explicit formula is a Fourier-type duality, and positivity on one side (pointwise or positive-definite) transfers to the other only as the OTHER kind of positivity. The Weil/HCF question needs pointwise-type positivity on the zero side, which corresponds to positive-definiteness of (arch + pole − prime), i.e. RH itself. No unconditional positivity of the right kind exists on the prime side. Holding continues. |
